\|u\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq C(M,E_{0})\left(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\Big(\int_{\Omega}\rho|u|\,{\rm d}{x}\Big)^{2}\right).

Remark 3.2
.

By integrating (
3.47
) over
[0,t],
we obtain for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+\eta w^{4}+w_{x}^{2}\bigr)(t)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl((\eta-1)^{2}+|w|^{2}|w_{x}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\eta w_{t}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta_{0}w_{0}^{2}+(\eta_{0}-1)^{2}+\eta_{0}w_{0}^{4}+w_{0,x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}<\infty,

from which and Lemma
3.6
, we infer

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+w_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Then along the same lines of proof of (
3.46
) , yet without using the positive lower bound of
\eta
,
we have

\bigl\|\bigl(\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}w,\eta-1,\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{2},w_{x}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}+\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{\rm h}}\leq CE_{10}^{\frac{1}{2}}(y)e^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}^{+},\ y\in\mathbb{R},

where
\alpha>0
solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0})
and
C>0
solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00})
.

3.6.
Decay estimate of
\|\eta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

From this subsection on,
we need to use the lower bound of
\eta
obtained in Proposition
3.3
.

Proposition 3.5
.

There exist positive constants
C
and
\alpha
depending solely on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0})
so that

(3.50)

\|\eta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}\leq CE_{10}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

Proof.

Let
\zeta\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\eta^{-1}.
By multiplying
-\eta^{-2}
to the density equation
of (
1.6
), we get

\displaystyle\zeta_{t}+w\zeta_{x}-\zeta w_{x}=0.

Applying
\partial_{x}
to the above equation gives

(3.51)

\zeta_{tx}+w\zeta_{xx}-\zeta w_{xx}=0\Longrightarrow\zeta w_{xx}=\zeta_{tx}+w\zeta_{xx}.

While we rewrite the momentum equation of (
1.6
) as

\displaystyle w_{t}+ww_{x}-\nu(\zeta_{tx}+w\zeta_{xx})-\zeta^{-1}p^{\prime}(\eta)\zeta_{x}=0.

Multiplying the above equation by
\eta
yeilds

(3.52)

\eta\big((w-\nu\zeta_{x})_{t}+w(w-\nu\zeta_{x})_{x}\big)-\eta^{2}p^{\prime}(\eta)\zeta_{x}=0.

By multiplying
(w-\nu\zeta_{x})
to (
3.52
) and integrating the resulting equation over
{\mathbb{T}},
we find

(3.53)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w-\nu\zeta_{x})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2}p^{\prime}(\eta)\zeta_{x}(w-\nu\zeta_{x})\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

We compute

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2}p^{\prime}(\eta)\zeta_{x}(w-\nu\zeta_{x})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\nu a\gamma\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{\gamma+1}\zeta_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{x}w\,{\rm d}{x}.

In view of Proposition
3.3
, we have
\eta\geq\underline{\eta}>0,
so that

\displaystyle\nu a\gamma\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{\gamma+1}\zeta_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\geq\nu a\gamma\underline{\eta}^{\gamma+1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

We observe that

\displaystyle\bigl|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{x}w\,{\rm d}{x}\bigl|

\displaystyle=\bigl|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)w_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}\bigl|=\bigl|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(p(\eta)-p(1))w_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}\bigl|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(p(\eta)-p(1))^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}\gamma^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2\gamma-2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta-1)^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By substituting the above estimates into (
3.53
), we achieve

(3.54)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w-\nu\zeta_{x})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu a\gamma\underline{\eta}^{\gamma+1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq a^{2}\gamma^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2\gamma-2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta-1)^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Let
A_{4}
be a large enough positive constant so that

(3.55)

A_{4}\geq 4+\nu\quad\hbox{and}\quad\frac{a}{2}A_{4}\geq\alpha^{2}\gamma^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2\gamma-2}+1.

Then by multiplying (
3.27
) by
A_{4}
and summing up the resulting inequality with (
3.54
), we obtain

(3.56)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}F_{3}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{x}^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+

\displaystyle\nu a\gamma\underline{\eta}^{\gamma+1}\zeta_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq 0\quad\hbox{with}\quad

\displaystyle F_{3}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\Bigl(\frac{A_{1}A_{4}}{2}\eta w^{2}+

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{4}\big(P(\eta)-P(1)-P^{\prime}(1)(\eta-1)\big)

\displaystyle-A_{4}(\eta w)I(\eta-1)+\frac{1}{2}\eta(w-\nu\zeta_{x})^{2}\Bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Notice that

\displaystyle 2a^{2}+(a-b)^{2}=a^{2}+2(a-\frac{b}{2})^{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{2}\geq a^{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{2},

by choosing
A_{4}
sufficiently large, we find

(3.57)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+\frac{\nu\underline{\eta}}{4}\zeta_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq F_{3}(t)\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\zeta_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x},

where
C
depends solely on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00})
.

It follows from (
3.56
)–(
3.57
) that

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}\leq CE_{10}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

with
\alpha
and
C
satisfying the assumptions in the proposition.
Then (
3.50
) follows from the fact that

\|\eta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}=\|\eta^{2}\zeta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\|\zeta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.5
.
∎

3.7.
Decay estimates of
H^{2}
norms

We first deduce from (
1.9
) that

(3.58)

E_{20}(y)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\|\varsigma_{0}-1\|_{H^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}+\|w_{0}\|_{H^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\bar{E}_{20}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}E_{20}(y)<\infty.

Lemma 3.7
.

We have

(3.59)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{xx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{10},

where
C
solely depends on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0})
.

Proof.

Indeed by multiplying
\eta^{-1}w_{xx}
to the momentum equation of (
1.6
) and integrating
the resulting equation over
{\mathbb{T}},
we find

(3.60)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\nu}{\bar{\eta}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{xx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\frac{4\bar{\eta}}{\nu}\Big(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w^{2}w_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+a^{2}\gamma^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2\gamma-4}\eta_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\Big).

Yet by virtue of (
3.46
) and (
3.50
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w^{2}w_{x}^{2}(t)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\|w(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\rm h}}^{2}\|w_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\leq CE_{10}e^{-\alpha t},

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2\gamma-4}\eta_{x}^{2}(t)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\underline{\eta}^{2\gamma-4}\|\eta_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\leq CE_{10}e^{-\alpha t},

where
C
solely depends on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0})
.
Then integrating (
3.60
) over
\mathbb{R}^{+}
leads to (
3.59
).
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
D_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\partial_{t}+w\partial_{x}
be the material derivative. Then there exist positive constants
C
and
\alpha
depending
on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10})
so that

(3.61)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{20}(y)e^{-\alpha t}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w)_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{20}(y).

Proof.

Applying the material derivative
D_{t}
to the momentum equation of (
1.6
) gives

\displaystyle\eta D_{t}^{2}w+D_{t}\eta D_{t}w-\nu D_{t}w_{xx}+D_{t}p(\eta)_{x}=0.

We compute

\displaystyle D_{t}\eta D_{t}w=-\eta w_{x}D_{t}w=-w_{x}(\nu w_{xx}-p(\eta)_{x})=-\frac{\nu}{2}(w_{x}^{2})_{x}+w_{x}p(\eta)_{x},

and

\displaystyle-\nu D_{t}w_{xx}=-\nu(w_{txx}+ww_{xxx})=-\nu(D_{t}w)_{xx}+\frac{3\nu}{2}(w_{x}^{2})_{x}.

As a result, it comes out

(3.62)

\displaystyle\eta D_{t}^{2}w-\nu(D_{t}w)_{xx}+\nu(w_{x}^{2})_{x}+\big(wp(\eta)_{x}\big)_{x}+p(\eta)_{tx}=0.

Multiplying (
3.62
) by
D_{t}w
and integrating the resulting equation over
{\mathbb{T}}
yields

(3.63)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w)_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{2}(D_{t}w)_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}wp(\eta)_{x}(D_{t}w)_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{t}(D_{t}w)_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w)_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+8\nu^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}^{4}\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta_{x}^{2}+w_{x}^{2})\,{\rm d}{x},

where
C
solely depend on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0})
, and we used the uniform boundedness of
\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}
.

While by applying Sobolev embedding theorem and Hölder inequality, one has

\displaystyle\|w_{x}\|_{L^{4}_{\rm h}}^{4}\leq\|w_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\|w_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\rm h}}^{2}\leq C\|w_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\|w_{x}\|_{H^{1}_{\rm h}}^{2}\leq Ce^{-2\alpha t}\big(\|w_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}+\|w_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{{\rm h}}}^{2}\big).

Then we deduce from (
3.63
) that there exists
C
solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10})
so that

(3.64)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w)_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{x}^{2}+w_{xx}^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

By multiplying (
3.60
) by a sufficiently large constant
A_{5}
and summing up the resulting inequality with (
3.64
), we get

(3.65)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(A_{5}w_{x}^{2}+\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{xx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w)_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{x}^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Observing that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{t}^{2}+\eta w_{x}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Then by virtue of (
3.47
) and (
3.56
), we can find a large enough constant
A_{6}
such that the quantity

(3.66)

\displaystyle F_{4}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}A_{6}F_{3}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(A_{5}w_{x}^{2}+\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+

\displaystyle\eta_{x}^{2}+\eta w_{x}^{2}+w_{x}^{2}+\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq F_{4}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}+\eta w_{x}^{2}+\eta|D_{t}w|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}F_{4}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{x}^{2}+(\eta-1)^{2}+|w|^{2}|w_{x}|^{2}+\eta w_{t}^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}+w_{xx}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq 0.

Here
F_{3}(t)
is defined in (
3.56
) and
C
solely depends on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10})
. Then there exists
\alpha>0
solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00},\underline{\varsigma}_{0})
such that

\displaystyle F_{4}(t)\leq CE_{20}e^{-\alpha t}.

And (
3.61
) follows.
∎

3.8.
Proof of Proposition
2.1

With the estimates obtained in the previous sections, we shall prove Proposition
2.1

by induction method and along the same line as that of Propositions
3.1
,

3.2
,
3.4
,
3.5

and
3.6
. Since it involves only technicalities, we postpone the proof in Appendix
A
.

4.
Decay estimates of
(\eta_{y},w_{y})

In this section, we investigate the decay in time estimates of
(\eta_{y},w_{y})
. We first get, by applying
\partial_{y}
to (
1.6
), that

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{yt}+(\eta w)_{yx}=0,\\
&(\eta w)_{yt}+(\eta w^{2})_{yx}-\nu w_{yxx}+p(\eta)_{yx}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Integrating (
4.1
) with respect to
x
over
{\mathbb{T}}
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

It follows from (
1.10
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\varsigma_{0y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\varsigma_{0}w_{0})_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

This implies

(4.2)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0,\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.

4.1.
Decay estimates of
L^{2}
norms

Without loss of generality, we may assume that

(4.3)

p^{\prime}(1)=1.

Note that this assumption (
4.3
) can always hold after a suitable normalization.
In view of (
1.9
), one has

(4.4)

E_{00}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\|(\partial_{y}\varsigma_{0},\partial_{y}w_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\bar{E}_{00}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}E_{00}^{(1)}<\infty.

Throughout this subsection,
A,\alpha
and
C
are positive numbers solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10},\underline{\varsigma}_{0}),
which may differ from line to line.

Lemma 4.1
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
one has

(4.5)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{00}^{(1)}.

Proof.

Taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy}_{2}
with
w_{y}
gives

(4.6)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yt}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{yx}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yx}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

Next we handle term by term above.
For the first term in (
4.6
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yt}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w_{y}+\eta_{y}w)_{t}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{t}w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}(ww_{y})_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{t}\eta_{y}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}.

For the second term in (
4.6
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{yx}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(2\eta ww_{y}+w^{2}\eta_{y})w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}.

And
for the last term in (
4.6
), one has

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yx}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By substituting the above equalities into (
4.6
), we achieve

(4.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{t}w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(ww_{x}\eta_{y}w_{y}+\eta w_{x}w_{y}^{2})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{t}\eta_{y}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta ww_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}.

On the other hand, by
taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy}_{1}
with
\eta_{y},
we find

(4.8)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yx}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w\eta_{yx}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(w_{x}\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta_{x}w_{y}\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yx}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Summing up (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) gives rise to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}+\eta w_{x}\big)w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(ww_{x}+w_{t}+\eta_{x}\big)\eta_{y}w_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}{(p^{\prime}(\eta)-\eta)}\eta_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta ww_{y}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x},

from which, (
4.3
), (
A.1
) and (
A.2
), we deduce

(4.9)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality leads to
(
4.5
).
∎

Proposition 4.1
.

We have

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)(t)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{00}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{00}^{(1)}(y).

Proof.

Recall (
3.8
), we get, by
multiplying the momentum equation of (
4.1
) by
I(\eta_{y})
and integrating the resulting equality over
{\mathbb{T}},
that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{y}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yt}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{y}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}.

It is easy to observe that

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}

\displaystyle(\eta w^{2})_{y}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w^{2}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}2\eta ww_{y}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x},

\displaystyle\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}\eta_{y}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

and

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yt}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{yt})\,{\rm d}{x}.

By virtue of (
4.2
), one has

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{yt})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I\left((\eta w)_{yx}\right)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}((\eta w)_{y}-(\eta w)(t,0,y))\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By inserting the above estimates into (
4.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2}w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Choosing
\delta=\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}
in the above inequality gives rise to

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{2}w_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Notice from Lemma
3.3
and (
4.2
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{y}^{2}-\langle\eta w_{y}\rangle^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{y}-\langle\eta w_{y}\rangle)^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

from which and
(
4.2
), we infer

(4.13)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{y}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\langle\eta_{y}w\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}w^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Hence thanks to (
3.46
), we deduce from (
4.12
) that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{y}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Let
A
be a sufficiently large constant, we denote

(4.15)

F_{1}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(A(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2})-(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Thanks to Lemma
4.1
, we get, by multiplying (
4.9
) by
A
and summing up the resulting inequality with (
4.14
), that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}F_{1}^{(1)}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{00}^{(1)}e^{-\alpha t}.

Due to

\displaystyle\bigl|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{y}I(\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}\bigr|\leq\|\eta_{y}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}\bigl(\|w\eta_{y}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}+\|\eta w_{y}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}\bigr)\leq(1+\|w\|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2})\,{\rm d}{x},

we deduce from (
4.13
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq F_{1}^{(1)}(t)\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x},

which together with (
4.16
) ensures (
4.10
).
∎

4.2.
Decay estimates of
H^{1}
norms

It follows from (
1.9
) that

(4.17)

E_{10}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\|\partial_{y}\varsigma_{0}\|_{H^{1}_{\rm h}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}w_{0}\|_{H^{1}_{\rm h}}^{2}\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\bar{E}_{10}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}E_{10}^{(1)}<\infty.

Throughout this subsection,
A,\alpha,C
are positive constants solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{00}^{(1)}),
which may differ from line to line.

Lemma 4.2
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, there holds

(4.18)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Proof.

We rewrite the equation
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy}_{2}
as

(4.19)

\eta(w_{yt}+ww_{yx}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta_{y}(w_{t}+ww_{x})-\nu w_{yxx}+p(\eta)_{yx}=0.

Multiplying the above equation by
\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}
and integrating the resulting equation over
{\mathbb{T}}
yields

(4.20)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(\eta(ww_{yx}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta_{y}(w_{t}+ww_{x})+p(\eta)_{yx}\big)\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Applying Young’s inequality and using (
A.2
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(\eta(ww_{yx}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta_{y}(w_{t}+ww_{x})+p(\eta)_{yx}\big)\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\left(w_{yx}^{2}+w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\right)\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|p(\eta)_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|p(\eta)_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{yx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

As a consequence, thanks to (
4.13
), we thus deduce (
4.18
) from (
4.20
).
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, there holds

(4.21)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{y}-\nu(\eta^{-1})_{yx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{-2}\eta_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Proof.

We rewrite the equation
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy}_{2}
as

(4.22)

D_{t}w_{y}+w_{x}w_{y}+\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}D_{t}w-\nu\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yx}=0.

Let
\zeta\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\eta^{-1}
. Then we deduce from equation
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\zeta w_{yxx}=(\zeta_{tx}+w\zeta_{xx})_{y}-\zeta_{y}w_{xx}=D_{t}\zeta_{yx}+w_{y}\zeta_{xx}-\zeta_{y}w_{xx}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{t}(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})+w_{x}w_{y}+\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}D_{t}w-\nu(w_{y}\zeta_{xx}-\zeta_{y}w_{xx})+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yx}=0.

By taking
L^{2}
inner product of the above equation with
\eta(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx}),
we find

(4.23)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yx}(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(\eta w_{x}w_{y}+\eta_{y}D_{t}w-\nu\eta(w_{y}\zeta_{xx}-\zeta_{y}w_{xx})\big)(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yx}(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{yx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x})(w_{y}-\nu(2\eta^{-3}\eta_{x}\eta_{y}-\eta^{-2}\eta_{yx}))\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{-2}\eta_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{yx}(w_{y}-2\nu\eta^{-3}\eta_{x}\eta_{y})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x}\bigl(w_{y}-2\nu\eta^{-3}\eta_{x}\eta_{y}+\nu\eta^{-2}\eta_{yx}\bigr))\,{\rm d}{x}.

Hence thanks to (
A.3
), we deduce from (
4.23
) that

(4.24)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{y}-\nu\zeta_{yx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{-2}\eta_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Choosing
\delta>0
small so that

\delta\leq\frac{\nu}{2}p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta}){\underline{\eta}}^{-2},

and using (
4.13
), we conclude the proof of (
4.21
).
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, there holds

(4.25)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\left(w_{yx}^{2}+\eta_{yx}^{2}\right)(t)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t},\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{10}^{(1)}(y),

and

(4.26)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yt}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{10}^{(1)}(y).

Proof.

Thanks to Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
, we deduce (
4.25
)
by similar arguments using in the proof of Proposition
3.5
.

While we get, by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
4.19
) with
w_{yt},
that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yt}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big(ww_{yx}+w_{y}w_{x}+\eta_{y}(w_{t}+ww_{x})+p(\eta)_{yx}\big)w_{yt}\,{\rm d}{x},

which implies

(4.27)

\displaystyle\nu\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yt}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta_{yx}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

By integrating (
4.27
) over
[0,t]
and using (
4.25
),
we obtain (
4.26
).
∎

4.3.
Decay estimate of
\|w_{y}\|_{H^{2}}

In view of (
1.9
), we have

(4.28)

E_{20}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\|\partial_{y}\varsigma_{0}\|_{H^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}w_{0}\|_{H^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\bar{E}_{20}^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}E_{20}^{(1)}<\infty.

Throughout this subsection,
A,\alpha,C
are positive numbers solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{20},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10}^{(1)}),
which may change from line to line.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
D_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\partial_{t}+w\partial_{x}
be the material derivative. Then for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, there holds

(4.29)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{y}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{20}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{y})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}t\leq CE_{20}^{(1)}(y),

and

(4.30)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|\eta_{yt}|^{2}+|w_{yt}|^{2}+|w_{yxx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE_{20}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Proof.

Applying
D_{t}
to
\eta\times
(
4.22
) gives

(4.31)

\displaystyle\eta D_{t}^{2}w_{y}+D_{t}\eta D_{t}w_{y}+D_{t}(\eta w_{x}w_{y})+D_{t}(\eta_{y}D_{t}w)-\nu D_{t}w_{yxx}+D_{t}p(\eta)_{yx}=0.

It is easy to observe that

\displaystyle D_{t}\eta D_{t}w_{y}

\displaystyle=-\eta w_{x}D_{t}w_{y}=w_{x}(\eta w_{x}w_{y}+\eta_{y}D_{t}w-\nu w_{yxx}+p(\eta)_{yx}),

\displaystyle D_{t}(\eta w_{x}w_{y})

\displaystyle=D_{t}(\eta w_{x})w_{y}+\eta w_{x}D_{t}w_{y},

\displaystyle D_{t}(\eta_{y}D_{t}w)

\displaystyle=-(\eta_{y}w_{x}+\eta_{x}w_{y}+\eta w_{yx})D_{t}w+\eta_{y}D_{t}^{2}w,

\displaystyle-\nu D_{t}w_{yxx}

\displaystyle=-\nu(D_{t}w_{y})_{xx}+\nu(2w_{x}w_{yxx}+w_{xx}w_{yx}),

and

\displaystyle D_{t}p(\eta)_{yx}=p(\eta)_{tyx}+wp(\eta)_{yxx}.

Then by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
4.31
) with
D_{t}w_{y}
and using the fact

w_{x}p(\eta)_{yx}+wp(\eta)_{yxx}=(wp(\eta)_{yx})_{x},

we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{y}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{y})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{y})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{y}^{2}+w_{yx}^{2}+w_{yxx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta_{yx}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

This implies

(4.32)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{y}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{y})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{y}^{2}+w_{yx}^{2}+w_{yxx}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta_{yx}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x},

from which,
Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
, Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
,
we deduce (
4.29
) by the similar argument used in the proof of Proposition
3.5
.

Thanks to (
4.29
), we conclude (
4.30
) by using the mass and momentum equations (
4.1
).
∎

4.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.2

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

By summarizing Propositions
4.1
,
4.2
and
4.3
,
we deduce that there exist two positive constants
C
and
\alpha
solely depends on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{20},\underline{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{10}^{(1)})
such that

(4.33)

\displaystyle\|\eta_{y}\|_{H^{1}_{\rm h}}+\|w_{y}\|_{H^{2}_{\rm h}}+\|\eta_{yt}\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}+\|w_{yt}\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}\leq C\left(E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

In what follows, we shall
follow the same strategy as that of the proof of Proposition
2.1
.
We first get, by applying
\partial_{x}
to (
4.1
), that

(4.34)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{yxt}+(\eta w)_{yxx}=0,\\
&(\eta w)_{yxt}+(\eta w^{2})_{yxx}-\nu w_{yxxx}+p(\eta)_{yxx}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

We can also rewrite
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy-dx}_{2}
as

(4.35)

\begin{split}D_{t}w_{yx}&+(w_{y}w_{xx}+2w_{x}w_{yx})+\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{x}+w_{x}^{2})\\
&+\eta^{-1}\eta_{x}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{x}w_{y})+\eta^{-1}\eta_{yx}D_{t}w-\nu\eta^{-1}w_{yxxx}+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yxx}=0.\end{split}

We split the proof of the remaining estimates in (
2.4
) into the following steps:

Step 1.
Decay estimates for
\eta_{yxx}.

Recalling that
\zeta\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\eta^{-1},

we deduce from
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy-dx}_{1}
that

(4.36)

\zeta w_{yxxx}=D_{t}\zeta_{yxx}-\zeta_{yx}w_{xx}-\zeta_{x}w_{yxx}-\zeta_{y}w_{xxx}+\zeta_{xxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{yxx}w_{x}+\zeta_{xx}w_{yx}.

Plugging (
4.36
) into (
4.35
) gives rise to

\displaystyle D_{t}(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})+w_{x}(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})+(w_{y}w_{xx}+w_{x}w_{yx})

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{x}+w_{x}^{2})+\eta^{-1}\eta_{x}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{x}w_{y})+\eta^{-1}\eta_{yx}D_{t}w

\displaystyle\quad-\nu\bigl(-\zeta_{yx}w_{xx}-\zeta_{x}w_{yxx}-\zeta_{y}w_{xxx}+\zeta_{xxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{xx}w_{yx}\bigr)+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yxx}=0.

By taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of the above equation with
\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})
and using the decay estimates we have derived in the previous sections, we obtain

(4.37)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})p(\eta)_{yxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(E_{20}(y)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle+C\delta^{-1}\left(E_{30}(y)+E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

To handle the term related to the pressure, we write

(4.38)

\displaystyle p(\eta)_{yxx}

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{yxx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{xx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)2\eta_{x}\eta_{yx}+p^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\eta)(-\zeta^{-2}\zeta_{yxx}+2\zeta^{-3}\zeta_{y}\zeta_{xx}+4\zeta^{-3}\zeta_{x}\zeta_{yx}-6\zeta^{-4}\zeta_{y}\zeta_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{xx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)2\eta_{x}\eta_{yx}+p^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{2}\nu^{-1}(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})-p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{2}\nu^{-1}w_{yx}+p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{3}(2\zeta_{y}\zeta_{xx}+4\zeta_{x}\zeta_{yx}-6\eta^{2}\zeta_{y}\zeta_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{xx}+p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)2\eta_{x}\eta_{yx}+p^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta)\eta_{y}\eta_{x}^{2}.

Notice that

p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta\geq p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})\underline{\eta}>0,

by choosing
\delta
sufficiently small, we deduce from (
4.37
) and (
4.38
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu^{-1}p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})\underline{\eta}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+C\left(E_{30}(y)+E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yx}-\nu\zeta_{yxx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{30}(y)+E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t},

from which and (
4.33
), we infer

(4.39)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{30}(y)+E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

This leads to

(4.40)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yxx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{30}(y)+E_{20}^{(1)}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

Step 2.
Decay estimates for
D_{t}w_{yx}.

We first rewrite
\eqref{CNS-1d-dy-dx}_{2}
as

(4.41)

\begin{split}&\eta D_{t}w_{yx}-\nu w_{yxxx}+\mathfrak{g}+p(\eta)_{yxx}=0,\\
\mathfrak{g}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\eta(w_{y}w_{xx}&+2w_{x}w_{yx})+\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{x}+w_{x}^{2})+\eta_{x}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{x}w_{y})+\eta_{yx}D_{t}w.\end{split}

By taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
4.41
) with
\eta^{-1}w_{yxxx},
we obtain

(4.42)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}w_{yx}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta^{-1}|w_{yxxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{g}+p(\eta)_{yxx}\bigr)\eta^{-1}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

It is easy to observe that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}w_{yx}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\partial_{t}w_{yx}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}ww_{yxx}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxx}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}(w_{yxx})^{2}.

While it follows from (
A.18
), (
4.33
) and (
4.40
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{g}+p(\eta)_{yxx}\bigr)\eta^{-1}w_{yxxx}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\delta^{-1}E(y)e^{-\alpha t}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By inserting the above estimates into (
4.42
) and choosing
\delta
suitably small, we achieve

(4.43)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\bar{\eta}^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

While by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
4.41
) with
D_{t}w_{yx},
we find

(4.44)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}w_{yxxx}D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(D_{t}\mathfrak{g}+D_{t}p(\eta)_{yxx}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

Observing that

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yxxx}D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yxxx}\partial_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yxxx}ww_{yxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(w_{yxx})^{2}w_{x}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

By using the estimates (
A.18
), (
4.33
) and (
4.40
), we find

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{g}+p(\eta)_{yxx}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\delta^{-1}E(y)e^{-\alpha t}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By substituting the above estimates into (
4.44
)
and taking
\delta
to be suitably small, we achieve

(4.45)

\displaystyle\nu\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

On the other hand, we get, by applying
D_{t}
to (
4.41
) and then taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of the resulting
equation with
D_{t}w_{yx},
that

(4.46)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(D_{t}(\eta D_{t}w_{yx})-\nu D_{t}w_{yxxx}+D_{t}\mathfrak{g}+D_{t}p(\eta)_{yxx}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

Next we handle term by term above. We first observe that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}(\eta D_{t}w_{yx})D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}\eta)D_{t}w_{yx}D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(D_{t}D_{t}w_{yx})D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}\eta)|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}.

Notice that

\displaystyle D_{t}w_{yxxx}=(D_{t}w_{yx})_{xx}-w_{xx}w_{yxx}-2w_{x}w_{yxxx},

we have

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}w_{yxxx}D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}w_{yx})_{xx}D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{xx}w_{yxx}+2w_{x}w_{yxxx}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\geq\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}+|w_{yxxx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

We get, by applying the estimates (
A.18
), (
4.33
) and (
4.40
), that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}\bigl(\mathfrak{g}-\eta_{x}D_{t}w_{y}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

In view of (
4.22
), we write

\displaystyle D_{t}(\eta_{x}D_{t}w_{y})=D_{t}\eta_{x}D_{t}w_{y}-\eta_{x}D_{t}\big(w_{x}w_{y}+\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}D_{t}w-\nu\eta^{-1}w_{yxx}+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yx}\big).

It suffices to deal with the highest order derivative term
\eta_{x}\eta^{-1}D_{t}w_{yxx}
above. Other terms can be estimated similarly, even easier.
Observing that

\displaystyle D_{t}w_{yxx}=(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}-w_{x}w_{yxx},

we get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{x}\eta^{-1}(D_{t}w_{yxx})D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\nu}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

For the term related to the pressure in (
4.46
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}p(\eta)_{yxx})D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\partial_{t}p(\eta)_{yxx}+wp(\eta)_{yxxx}\bigr)D_{t}w_{yx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl((\partial_{t}p(\eta)_{yx}+wp(\eta)_{yxx})(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}-w_{x}p(\eta)_{yxx}D_{t}w_{yx}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\partial_{t}p(\eta)_{yx}+wp(\eta)_{yxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\nu}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|w_{x}p(\eta)_{yxx}|^{2}+|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\nu}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By substituting the above estimate into (
4.46
), we arrive at

(4.47)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|w_{yxxx}|^{2}+|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

By virtue of (
4.43
), (
4.45
) and (
4.47
), we can find a large enough positive constant
A>0
so that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(A\bigl(|w_{yxx}|^{2}+|w_{yxx}|^{2}\bigr)+\eta|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|w_{yxxx}|^{2}+|D_{t}w_{yx}|^{2}+\nu|(D_{t}w_{yx})_{x}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Together with (
4.33
), we deduce from (
4.48
) that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\|D_{t}w_{yx}\|_{L^{2}_{h}}^{2}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Together with (
4.33
), this implies

(4.50)

\displaystyle\|w_{yxxx}\|_{L^{2}_{h}}^{2}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

By summarizing the estimates (
4.33
), (
4.40
), (
4.49
) and (
4.50
), we conclude the proof of (
2.4
).
∎

5.
Decay estimates of
(\eta_{yy},w_{yy})

In this section, we investigate the decay in time estimates of
(\eta_{y},w_{y})
. We first get,
by applying
\partial_{y}^{2}
to (
1.6
), that

(5.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{yyt}+(\eta w)_{yyx}=0,\\
&(\eta w)_{yyt}+(\eta w^{2})_{yyx}-\nu w_{yyxx}+p(\eta)_{yyx}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Integrating (
5.1
) with respect to
x
over
{\mathbb{T}}
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

This implies

(5.2)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}=0,\quad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.

5.1.
Decay estimates of
L^{2}
norms

Introduce

(5.3)

E_{00}^{(2)}(y)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\|(\partial_{y}^{2}\varsigma_{0},\partial_{y}^{2}w_{0})(y)\|_{L^{2}_{\rm h}}^{2}\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\bar{E}_{00}^{(2)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}E_{00}^{(2)}<\infty.

Throughout this subsection,
A,\alpha
and
C
are positive numbers solely depending on
(a,\gamma,\nu,\bar{\varsigma}_{0},\bar{E}_{20},\bar{E}_{10}^{(1)},\underline{\varsigma}_{0}),
which may differ from line to line. We first give the basic energy estimates:

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
one has

(5.4)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right).

Proof.

Firstly, we rewrite
\eqref{CNS-1d-dyy}_{2}
as

(5.5)

\displaystyle\eta D_{t}w_{yy}+\eta(2w_{y}w_{yx}+w_{yy}w_{x})+2\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta_{yy}D_{t}w-\nu w_{yyxx}+p(\eta)_{yyx}=0.

By using the decay estimates we have derived in the previous sections and testing (
5.5
) by
w_{yy}
, we obtain

(5.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta_{yy}w_{yyx}+CE_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

On the other hand, by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of
\eqref{CNS-1d-dyy}_{1}
with
\eta_{yy},
we find

(5.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yyx}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yyx}+\eta_{yy}w_{x}+\eta_{yyx}w+2\eta_{yx}w_{y}+2\eta_{y}w_{yx}+\eta w_{yyx}+\eta_{x}w_{yy}\bigr)\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yyx}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{yy}w_{x}+2\eta_{yx}w_{y}+2\eta_{y}w_{yx}+\eta w_{yyx}+\eta_{x}w_{yy}\bigr)\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By summing up (
5.6
) with (
5.7
), we achieve

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\nu}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(p^{\prime}(\eta)-\eta)\eta_{yy}w_{yyx}\,{\rm d}{x}+CE_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t},

from which and the decay of
p^{\prime}(\eta)-\eta
obtained in (
2.3
), we infer

(5.8)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+CE_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality leads to (
5.4
).
∎

Proposition 5.1
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)(t)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

Proof.

We first get, by multiplying the momentum equation of (
5.1
) by
I(\eta_{yy})
and integrating the resulting equality over
{\mathbb{T}},
that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yy}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yyt}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{yy}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}.

We now handle term by term above. For the term on the left-hand side of (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p(\eta)_{yy}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)(\eta_{yy})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime\prime}(\eta)(\eta_{y})^{2}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)(\eta_{yy})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-C\delta E_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta_{yy})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-C\delta^{-1}E_{10}^{(1)}(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

We then compute the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.10
) as follows

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yyt}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yyt})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I\left((\eta w)_{yyx}\right)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}((\eta w)_{yy}(t,x,y)-(\eta w)(t,0,y))\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}((\eta w)_{yy})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta_{yy}w+2\eta_{y}w_{y}+\eta w_{yy})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

where we used
\eqref{CNS-1d-dyy}_{1}
, (
5.2
), (
5.4
), and the decay estimates we have obtained in the previous sections.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w^{2})_{yy}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(w^{2}\eta_{yy}+2\eta_{y}ww_{y}+\eta(ww_{yy}+w_{y}^{2}))\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

For the last term in (
5.10
), we find

\displaystyle\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}\eta_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\delta\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\delta^{-1}\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Notice that
p^{\prime}(\eta)\geq p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})>0
, by substituting the above estimates into (
5.10
)
and taking
\delta
to be suitably small, we achieve

(5.11)

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}+C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+w_{yyx}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

While it follows from a similar proof of Lemma
3.3
that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}-\langle\eta w_{yy}\rangle^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

In view of (
5.2
), it holds that

\langle\eta w_{yy}\rangle=-\langle w\eta_{yy}\rangle-\langle 2\eta_{y}w_{y}\rangle.

As a result, we infer

(5.12)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta w_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\langle\eta w_{yy}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+8\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta_{yy}^{2}w^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}w_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Thanks to (
5.11
) and (
5.12
), we deduce that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{yy}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyx}^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Let
A
be a sufficiently large positive constant, we denote

(5.14)

F_{1}^{(2)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(A(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2})-(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\quad\hbox{and}\quad F_{2}^{(2)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}F_{1}^{(1)}+F_{1}^{(2)},

where
F_{1}^{(1)}
is given in (
4.15
).

Then by virtue of (
5.8
) and (
5.13
), we get

(5.15)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}F_{1}^{(2)}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yyx}^{2}+\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

Notice that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta w)_{yy}I(\eta_{yy})\,{\rm d}{x}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\eta w)_{yy}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}\|\eta_{yy}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta_{yy}w+2\eta_{y}w_{y}+\eta w_{yy}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}\|\eta_{yy}\|_{L^{1}_{\rm h}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+w_{y}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Then by (
4.16
) and (
5.15
), we infer

(5.17)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}F_{2}^{(2)}(t)+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{yx}^{2}+w_{yyx}^{2}+\frac{p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})}{2}(\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq C\left(E_{10}^{(1)}(y)+E_{20}(y)\right)e^{-\alpha t}.

On the other hand, in view of (
4.13
), (
5.12
) and (
5.16
), by choosing
A
suitably large, we deduce that

(5.18)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq F_{2}^{(2)}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\eta w_{y}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+\eta w_{yy}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(w_{xy}^{2}+\eta_{y}^{2}+w_{yyx}^{2}+\eta_{yy}^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

Our desired estimate (
5.9
) follows from (
5.17
) and (
5.18
).
∎

5.2.
Proof of Proposition
2.3

Proof of Proposition
2.3
.

We divide the proof into the following two steps:

Step 1.
Decay estimate of
\eta_{yyx}.

The idea to derive the decay estimate of
\eta_{yyx}
is similar as that of
\eta_{yxx}
in Section
4.4
.
In what follows, we just outline its derivation. In order to do so, wfirst e rewrite
\eqref{CNS-1d-dyy}_{2}
as

(5.19)

\displaystyle D_{t}w_{yy}+(2w_{y}w_{yx}+w_{yy}w_{x})+\eta^{-1}\bigl(2\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta_{yy}D_{t}w-\nu w_{yyxx}+p(\eta)_{yyx}\bigr)=0.

While it follows from
\eqref{CNS-1d-dyy}_{1}
that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\zeta w_{yyxx}=D_{t}\zeta_{yyx}-\zeta_{yy}w_{xx}-\zeta_{y}w_{yxx}-\zeta_{y}w_{yxx}+\zeta_{yxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{yxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{xx}w_{yy}.

Plugging (
5.20
) into (
5.19
) gives

(5.21)

\displaystyle D_{t}(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})+\eta^{-1}p(\eta)_{yyx}+\mathfrak{g}_{1}=0,

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}=

\displaystyle(2w_{y}w_{yx}+w_{yy}w_{x})+2\eta^{-1}\eta_{y}(D_{t}w_{y}+w_{y}w_{x})+\eta^{-1}\eta_{yy}D_{t}w

\displaystyle-\nu(-\zeta_{yy}w_{xx}-\zeta_{y}w_{yxx}-\zeta_{y}w_{yxx}+\zeta_{yxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{yxx}w_{y}+\zeta_{xx}w_{yy}).

Then we deduce from Propositions
2.1
,
2.2
and
5.1
that

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{h}}^{2}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

By taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
5.21
) with
\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})
and using
(
5.22
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\zeta^{-2}\zeta_{yxx}(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{-1}E(y)e^{-\alpha t}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

from which, we infer

(5.23)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle+\nu^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}p^{\prime}(\eta)\eta^{2}(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{-1}E(y)e^{-\alpha t}+\delta\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Taking
\delta=(2\nu)^{-1}p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})\underline{\eta}
in (
5.23
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu^{-1}p^{\prime}(\underline{\eta})\underline{\eta}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t},

from which, we infer

(5.24)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(w_{yy}-\nu\zeta_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t},

and

(5.25)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\zeta_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\eta_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Step 2.
Decay estimates of
D_{t}w_{yy}.

The main idea to derive the decay estimates of
D_{t}w_{yy}
is analogues to that of
D_{t}w_{yx}

in Section sec-decay-y-all. We shall outline its proof below.

Observing that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}w_{yy}w_{yyxx}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\partial_{t}w_{yy}w_{yyxx}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}ww_{yyx}w_{yyxx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{x}(w_{yyx})^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Then by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
5.5
) with
\eta^{-1}w_{yyxx},
we deduce that

(5.26)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\bar{\eta}^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyxx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

While due to

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyxx}D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyxx}\partial_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}w_{yyxx}ww_{yyx}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(w_{yyx})^{2}w_{x}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

we get, by taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
5.5
) with
D_{t}w_{yy},
that

(5.27)

\displaystyle\nu\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|w_{yyx}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}D_{t}(\eta D_{t}w_{yy})D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}\eta)D_{t}w_{yy}D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta(D_{t}^{2}w_{yy})D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}\eta)|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}-C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

and

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}w_{yyxx})D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=-\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(D_{t}w_{yy})_{xx}D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\nu(w_{xx}w_{yyx}+2w_{x}w_{yyxx})D_{t}w_{yy}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\geq\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yy})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}-C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}+|w_{yyxx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x},

we get, by applying
D_{t}
to (
5.5
) and then taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of the resulting equation with
D_{t}w_{yy},

that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\nu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|(D_{t}w_{yy})_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}+C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|w_{yyxx}|^{2}+|D_{t}w_{yy}|^{2}+|w_{yyx}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}.

By virtue of (
5.15
)–(
5.18
), we deduce from (
5.26
), (
5.27
) and (
5.28
) that

(5.29)

\displaystyle\|D_{t}w_{yy}\|_{L^{2}_{h}}^{2}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t},

from which and (
5.1
), we infer

(5.30)

\displaystyle\|w_{yyxx}\|_{L^{2}_{h}}^{2}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

By summarizing the estimates (
5.9
), (
5.25
), (
5.29
) and
(
5.30
), we conclude the proof of (
2.5
).
∎

6.
Decay estimates of
\mathfrak{w}

With the decay estimates for
(\eta,w)
obtained in the previous sections,
we are going to derive the same exponential decay estimates for
\mathfrak{w}
.
Notice that the equation for
\mathfrak{w},
(
1.7
), is of standard parabolic type, we shall only
present the main decay in time estimates of
\mathfrak{w}
and skip the derivation of the related estimates
concerning
(\partial_{y}\mathfrak{w},\partial_{y}^{2}\mathfrak{w}).

The main results state as follows

Proposition 6.1
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, one has

(6.1a)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\underline{\eta}^{-1}e^{-\mu\bar{\eta}^{-2}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{0}|\mathfrak{w}_{0}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

(6.1b)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t},

(6.1c)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\left(|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|+|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+|\partial_{xx}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\right)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Proof.

1) We first get, by
taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
1.7
) with
\mathfrak{w}
and using integrating by parts, that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\mu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

While observing from the density equation of (
1.6
) and (
1.7
) that

\displaystyle(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{t}+(\eta w\mathfrak{w})_{x}-\mu\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{w}=0.

Integrating the above equation over
{\mathbb{T}}
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta\mathfrak{w})\,{\rm d}{x}=0,

which together with (
1.10
) ensures that

(6.3)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta\mathfrak{w})\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{0}\mathfrak{w}_{0}\,{\rm d}{x}=0.

Then we get, by using a similar proof of Lemma
3.3
, that

(6.4)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\bar{\eta}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By virtue of (
6.4
), we deduce from (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq e^{\mu\bar{\eta}^{-2}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta_{0}|\mathfrak{w}_{0}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x},

which together with (
3.30
) ensures (
6.1a
).

2) By taking
L^{2}
inner product of (
1.7
) with
D_{t}\mathfrak{w}
and using integrating by
parts, we obtain

(6.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\mu}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq\frac{\mu}{2}\|w_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

By multiplying (
6.2
) by
\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}1+\|w_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\Omega)}
and summing up the
resulting inequality with (
6.5
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl({\mathfrak{A}_{1}}\eta|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+{\mu}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\mathfrak{A}_{1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\mu|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\leq 0.

Due to
D_{t}\mathfrak{w}=\frac{\mu}{\eta}\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{w},
(
6.1b
) follows.

3)
Applying
D_{t}
to (
1.7
) gives

\displaystyle D_{t}(\eta D_{t}\mathfrak{w})-\mu D_{t}\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{w}=0.

We observe that

\displaystyle D_{t}(\eta D_{t}\mathfrak{w})=(-\eta\partial_{x}w)D_{t}\mathfrak{w}+\eta D_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{w},

and

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{w}=\partial_{x}^{2}(D_{t}\mathfrak{w})-\partial_{x}(\partial_{x}w\partial_{x}\mathfrak{w}).

As a result, it comes out

(6.6)

\displaystyle\eta D_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{w}-\mu\partial_{x}^{2}(D_{t}\mathfrak{w})=(\eta\partial_{x}w)D_{t}\mathfrak{w}-\mu\partial_{x}(\partial_{x}w\partial_{x}\mathfrak{w}).

By taking
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
inner product of (
6.6
) with
D_{t}\mathfrak{w}
, we find

(6.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\mu\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\partial_{x}(D_{t}\mathfrak{w})|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\eta\partial_{x}w)|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\mu(\partial_{x}w\partial_{x}\mathfrak{w})\partial_{x}D_{t}\mathfrak{w}\,{\rm d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}w\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\partial_{x}w\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\partial_{x}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}+\frac{\mu}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}|\partial_{x}(D_{t}\mathfrak{w})|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}.

Multiplying (
6.5
) by
\mathfrak{A}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}2+2\|\partial_{x}w\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\Omega)}^{2}
and summing up the resulting inequality with (
6.7
) yields

(6.8)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{A}_{1}\mathfrak{A}_{2}\eta|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+\mu A_{2}|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}+\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(\mu|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|^{2}+\eta|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+\mu|\partial_{x}(D_{t}\mathfrak{w})|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq 0.

Then by using a similar proof of Lemma
3.3
and Gronwall’s inequality, we find

(6.9)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\bigl(|\mathfrak{w}|^{2}+|\mathfrak{w}_{x}|+|D_{t}\mathfrak{w}|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

Observing that
\mu\partial_{xx}\mathfrak{w}=\eta D_{t}\mathfrak{w},
we conclude the proof of (
6.1c
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

Let us now outline the proof of Proposition
2.4
.

Proof of Proposition
2.4
.

Along the same line to proof of Proposition
6.1
and through the induction method as what we used in
the Appendix
A
, we deduce that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{w}(t)\|_{H^{5}_{\rm h}}+\|\mathfrak{w}_{t}(t)\|_{H^{3}_{\rm h}}+\|\mathfrak{w}_{tt}(t)\|_{H^{1}_{\rm h}}\leq CE(y)e^{-\alpha t}.

The decay estimates related to
y
-derivatives of
\mathfrak{w}
in (
2.6
) can be derived along the same line. We omit the details here.
∎

7.
Energy estimates for the perturbed equations

The purpose of this section is to present the proof of Propositions
2.6
and
2.7
.
For simplicity, we shall neglect the subscript
\varepsilon
in the rest of this section.

7.1.
Basic energy estimate

In this subsection, we shall derive a basic energy estimate for all
t<T^{\star}.
We first deduce from Proposition
2.5

that

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(\rho,u)
and
(\rho^{\rm a},u^{\rm a})
be respectively given by (
2.7
) and (
2.11
). Then one has

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigl((\rho,u)|(\rho^{\rm a},u^{\rm a})\bigr)(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\bigl(\mu\left|\nabla(u-u^{\rm a})\right|^{2}+\mu^{\prime}|{\rm div\,}(u-u^{\rm a})|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}(t^{\prime})\,{\rm d}t^{\prime},

where for
G
given by (
2.9
),

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}\Bigl((\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a})+\rho(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}

\displaystyle+\rho(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}G\Bigr)\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\bigl(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})-p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Proof.

Since
(\rho,u)
and
(\rho^{\rm a},u^{\rm a})
have the same initial data, we get, by
applying Proposition
7.1
, that (
7.1
) holds with

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}\Bigl(

\displaystyle\rho\mathfrak{D}_{t}u^{\rm a}\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)+\mu\nabla u^{\rm a}:\nabla(u^{\rm a}-u)+\mu^{\prime}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\,{\rm div\,}(u^{\rm a}-u)

\displaystyle+(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\eta)+(\rho^{\rm a}u^{\rm a}-\rho u)\cdot\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})-{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))\Bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

It follows from the
u^{\rm a}
equation of (
2.8
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}\rho\bigl(\partial_{t}u^{\rm a}+u^{\rm a}\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}+(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}\bigr)\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\rho\Bigl((\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}\bigl(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}-\nabla p(\rho^{\rm a})+G\bigr)+(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}\Bigr)(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

By using integration by parts and the fact that
P^{\prime\prime}(s)=s^{-1}p^{\prime}(s)
, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\bigl((\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\bigl(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\bigr)-\rho\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})+\rho(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}G\bigr)\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\bigl(\mu\nabla u^{\rm a}:\nabla(u^{\rm a}-u)+\mu^{\prime}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\,{\rm div\,}(u^{\rm a}-u)\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\rho(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Plugging the above equality into (
7.3
) gives

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\Bigl((\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\bigl(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\bigr)+\rho(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}G+(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}

\displaystyle-\rho\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\Bigr)\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\bigl((\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\eta)+(\rho^{\rm a}u^{\rm a}-\rho u)\cdot\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Notice that

P^{\prime\prime}(s)=s^{-1}p^{\prime}(s)
and the renormalized equation

(7.5)

\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})+{\rm div\,}(u^{\rm a}P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a}))+(P^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\rho^{\rm a}-P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}=\varepsilon P^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}.

We get, by using the continuity equation
\eqref{CNS-1d-W}_{1}
, that

(7.6)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle-\rho\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)+(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\eta)+(\rho^{\rm a}u^{\rm a}-\rho u)\cdot\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)\left(\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})+u^{\rm a}\cdot\nabla P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\right)

\displaystyle=(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)\left(\left(\partial_{t}P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})+{\rm div\,}(u^{\rm a}P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a}))+(P^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\rho^{\rm a}-P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\right)-P^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\rho^{\rm a}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\right)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}-(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a}){\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}.

Then (
7.2
) follows by inserting (
7.6
) into (
7.4
).
∎

We now present the proof of Proposition
2.6
.

Proof of Proposition
2.6
.

We first get, by applying (
2.15
) and Taylor’s expansion, that for
t<T^{\star},

(7.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigl((\rho,u)|(\rho^{\rm a},u^{\rm a})\bigr)(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}\rho|u-u^{\rm a}|^{2}+P(\rho)-P(\rho^{\rm a})-P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\Bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\Omega}\bigl({\underline{\eta}}/{4}|R|^{2}+\gamma(2\bar{\eta})^{\gamma-2}|\varrho|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

While it follows from Lemma
7.1
that

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\bigl(\mu\left|\nabla R\right|^{2}+\mu^{\prime}|{\rm div\,}R|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}(t^{\prime})\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}.

According to (
7.2
), we decompose
\mathcal{R}
as

\mathcal{R}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\sum_{j=1}^{5}\mathcal{R}_{j}(t).

We first deduce from Theorem
1.1
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a})\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|(\mu\Delta u^{\rm a}+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\varrho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|R\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\bigl(\|\varrho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|R\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\bigr).

For the second term in (
7.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}G\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|G\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|R\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

which together with (
2.10
) and Theorem
1.1
ensures that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(t)\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|R\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

For the third term in (
7.2
), we get, by applying Theorem
1.1
, that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\int_{\Omega}\rho(u-u^{\rm a})\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}\cdot(u^{\rm a}-u)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla u^{\rm a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{\Omega}\rho|R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\mathcal{E}_{1}(t).

Along the same line and thanks to (
2.17
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}-\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\bigl(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})-p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq(\gamma-1)\|{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{\Omega}\bigl(P(\rho)-P(\rho^{\rm a})-P^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})(\rho-\rho^{\rm a})\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\leq Ce^{-\alpha t}\mathcal{E}_{1}(t),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{5}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\hbox{\footnotesize def}}}\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{\rm a}-\rho)(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\rho^{\rm a})^{-1}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\varrho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\alpha t}\|\varrho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

By inserting the above estimates into (
7.8
) and using (
7.7
),
we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mu\left|\nabla R\right|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\alpha t^{\prime}}\bigl(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1}(t^{\prime})^{1/2}+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t^{\prime})\bigr)\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}.

Applying Gornwall’s inequality gives rise to

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mu\left|\nabla R\right|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\leq C\varepsilon,

which together with (
7.7
) ensures (
2.21
). This completes the proof of Proposition
2.6
.
∎

7.2.
Estimates of
\nabla R

Lemma 7.2
.

For each
t<T^{\star}
, there holds

(7.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{D}_{t}R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{3}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+C\varepsilon.

Proof.

We first get, by taking
L^{2}
inner product of the
R
equation of (
2.13
) with
\mathfrak{D}_{t}R
, that

(7.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathfrak{D}_{t}R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}\bigl(\mu\Delta R+\mu^{\prime}\nabla{\rm div\,}R\bigr)\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\int_{\Omega}\nabla\big(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})\big)\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\Bigl(\rho R\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}+\varrho\bigl(\partial_{t}u^{\rm a}+u^{\rm a}\cdot\nabla u^{\rm a}\bigr)-G\Bigr)\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=0.

Let us now handle term by term above. By using integration by parts, we find

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\Delta R\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\int_{\Omega}\nabla R:\bigl(\nabla u\nabla R+(u\cdot\nabla)\nabla R\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Due to
u=u^{\rm a}+R,
one has

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\nabla R:(\nabla u\nabla R)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\nabla R:\bigl(\nabla u^{\rm a}\nabla R\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\int_{\Omega}\nabla R:(\nabla R\nabla R)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla u^{\rm a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{3}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y},

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\nabla R:((u\cdot\nabla)\nabla R)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}({\rm div\,}u^{\rm a})|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\,\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}({\rm div\,}R)|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

This together with Theorem
1.1
ensures that

(7.11)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\Delta R\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{3}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Exactly along the same line, one has

(7.12)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\nabla{\rm div\,}R\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}|{\rm div\,}R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle-Ce^{-\alpha t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}|\nabla R|^{3}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

It is a little trickier to deal with the pressure term in (
7.10
). Indeed we first observe that

(7.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\nabla(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=-\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}(u\cdot\nabla R)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

It follows from the continuity equations of (
1.1
) and (
2.8
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))

\displaystyle=-{\rm div\,}\big(p(\rho)u-p(\rho^{\rm a})u^{\rm a}\big)-a(\gamma-1)\big(\rho^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\big)-\varepsilon p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}

so that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=\int_{\Omega}\big(p(\rho)u-p(\rho^{\rm a})u^{\rm a}\big)\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle-a(\gamma-1)\int_{\Omega}\big(\rho^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\big){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

While we observe that

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}(u\cdot\nabla R)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=-\int_{\Omega}\bigl(p(\rho)u-p(\rho^{\rm a})u^{\rm a}\bigr)\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{\Omega}p(\rho^{\rm a})R\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))(\nabla u^{\rm a}+\nabla R):\nabla R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

As a result, it comes out

(7.14)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\nabla(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))\cdot\mathfrak{D}_{t}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}=-\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a})){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle-a(\gamma-1)\int_{\Omega}\big(\rho^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\big){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}p^{\prime}(\rho^{\rm a})[(\eta\mathfrak{w})_{y}]_{\varepsilon}{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}p(\rho^{\rm a})R\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}-\int_{\Omega}(p(\rho)-p(\rho^{\rm a}))(\nabla u^{\rm a}+\nabla R):\nabla R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\big(\rho^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\big){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\big(\rho^{\gamma}{\rm div\,}R+(\rho^{\gamma}-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma}){\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\big){\rm div\,}R\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\rho}^{\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|{\rm div\,}R|^{2}\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y}+\|{\rm div\,}u^{\rm a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{\Omega}\bigl((\rho^{\gamma}-(\rho^{\rm a})^{\gamma})^{2}+|{\rm div\,}R|^{2}\bigr)\,{\rm d}{x}\,{\rm d}{y},

and
